### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月 ISM 非製造業 PMI 從 8 月的 55.4 降至 54.9，顯示服務業活動增速放
緩，然而國內需求強勁使供應鏈承壓，並推動投入價格指數由 72.6 升至 74.0 為
四年多來新高；另一方面，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心指數由 9 月的 48.1
降至 46.3，為連續第三個月下降且創 5 月以來最低 ， 惟未來一年通膨預期由 4.6%
升至 4.7%，顯示通膨可能持續維持高位至 2027 年。
⚫ 聯準會 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示與會官員均支持升息，並認為年底前尚需升息
一次 ，但對於後續升息時點及升息理由上存在分歧； 其中部分官員認為升息是防
止能源及其他價格衝擊造成更廣泛影響 的預防性措施，但更為鷹派官員則認為
升息在於抑制投資和支出，以防範正在顯現的需求驅動型通膨。
⚫ 歐洲央行 9 月貨幣政策會議紀要顯示全體決策委員均同意升息，並認為當前經
濟成長風險偏向下行 ， 通膨風險則偏向上行 ，因此將保留政策彈性， 根據經濟數

據做為決策，不提供未來利率路徑指引。
⚫ 台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨警
戒線 ， 且創兩年半來最大漲幅 ，除了豪雨導致蔬果價格上漲及中秋假期的短期因
素外，油價居高不下亦使通膨壓力增加。
⚫ 中國央行發布匯率政策立場聲明， 強調中國貿易發展根源於產業國際競爭力的
提升，無必要通過匯率貶值獲取貿易競爭優勢 ，並指出中國長期實施以市場供需
為基礎、參考一籃子貨幣進行調節管理浮動匯率制度。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.699，低點在 31.940，以 31.875 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 4.5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，受到前週公布的美國非農就業數據低於預期，降低
市場對聯準會 10 月升息的預期， 台股創高吸引外資匯入 ， 推升台幣最高來到31.699；
不過隨著美伊緊張情勢升溫且台灣連假將至 ， 台股收低使外資轉為大幅賣超並匯出 ，
推低台幣回到 9 月中以來的震盪區間下緣，最低觸及 31.940，最終在央行調節之下
收在 31.875。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心
指數持續下滑，由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期
升至 4.7%，加上中東局勢反覆，油價高位震盪，仍支撐市場對於聯準會年內升息
的預期，預計美元指數維持在 102 附近震盪，對台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台灣 9 月出口創歷史新高，年增 60.9%，反映 AI 及科技需求持續強勁，出口商後
續拋匯需求可望提供台幣支撐；然而，台股逼近五萬點關卡之際，外資未能延續買
超走勢，且連假前已大幅賣超台股，若後續外資持續匯出，將限制台幣升值空間。
綜合以上，預估本週台幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.700-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣區間震盪 。 中國十一長假期間 (10/1~10/7) 境內人民幣市場休市 ， 而
境外人民幣維持偏強格局 ， 主要在6.7000 上方震盪 ， 臨近周末 ， 人行中間價為6.7330，
創 3 年多來新高，加上美元指數漲勢放緩，推升境外人民幣突破 6.7000 整數關卡，
終場境外人民幣收在 6.6927，週線升值 134 點，境內人民幣收在 6.6929，較十一長
假前夕升值 121 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間偏升。就國際市場來看，美國密西根大學消費者信心指數
由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創5 月以來新低 ， 不過對於未來一年的通膨預期升至4.7%，
加上油價維持高檔 ， 市場對於聯準會年內升息的預期仍存 ， 美元指數維持高檔震盪 ，
將限制人民幣升值速度；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價維持偏強設定，不過人
行表示將防範短期大幅波動與急貶風險 ， 並無意透過人民幣貶值取得貿易競爭優勢 ，
有助於穩定市場對人民幣的預期 ；最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中 ， 各天期
均線仍向下發散，空頭格局不變，下方支撐先看向 9 月底低點 6.6908。綜合以上，
預估本週人民幣區間偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7100

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪走升。週初，因美元指數在102 附近盤整，帶領USD/JPY 延
續前一週走勢震盪走升到 158 上方；週間，因缺乏重要經濟數據，市場偏向觀望，
USD/JPY 維持在 158 附近震盪；臨近週末，USD/JPY 曾一度回測 157.50 附近，不
過在美日利差的主導下，USD/JPY 依舊保持偏多的走勢，終場收在 158.31，週線上
漲 84 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、路透短觀調查顯示，受半導
體強勁訂單推動，日本 10 月大型製造業信心升至正 22，創近五年新高；但原料成
本高漲重擊非製造業，信心指數降至正 23。與此同時，此前投下升息反對票的日銀
鴿派委員佐藤綾野立場軟化，表態支持分階段升息，市場對後續正常化預期升溫，
預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間 。二 、 日本首相高市早苗表示密切關注匯率與物價
走勢 ， 強調日本已脫離通縮 ， 將完全尊重央行政策獨立性 ， 暗示不反對進一步升息 。
面對通膨加劇與日圓走貶壓力，其過往堅持的貨幣寬鬆立場已出現轉向。然而目前
美日實質利差仍大，持續為 USD/JPY 提供支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，
USD/JPY 持續沿著上彎的月線上攻，然匯價持續承壓於季線下方，在有效站上季線
及突破前高壓力前，仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震
盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：157.10-159.50。 
前週交易區間 
157.41 158.50

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 震盪 走跌 。週初， 因法國債券風險與區域政治不確定性 ，
EUR/USD 下行最低來到 1.1160； 週間 ，受中東地緣風險升溫與歐洲財政疑慮帶動避
險需求 ， 加上美債殖利率維持高檔 ， 美元利差優勢延續，歐元維持低檔； 臨近週末 ，
逢低歐元有買盤支撐，帶動歐元收復部分跌幅，終場收在 1.1201，週線下跌 46 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學
家連恩表示，夏末能源成本飆升為經濟增長帶來下行風險，並可能限制央行收緊貨
幣政策的力道，與此同時，歐元區 10 月 Sentix 投資者信心指數由 5.1 降至 2.7，德
國預期指數同步滑落，反映市場對經濟復甦的信心明顯降溫，預計將令歐元持續承
壓。二、法國社會動盪與支出訴求阻礙財政減赤進程，加劇債務可持續性疑慮，並
推升法德公債利差與信用風險，令歐元承壓走弱。相較美國經濟展現韌性且聯準會
維持偏鷹立場，歐洲深陷景氣放緩與財政掣肘，歐洲央行緊縮空間受限。在歐美基
本面與政策前景持續分歧下 ，EUR/USD 缺乏實質支撐 ， 後市將維持偏弱整理並下探
關鍵支撐。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖多次向下測試 1.1165 關
鍵價位均獲得支撐，惟每次反彈均無法站上五日線，且各天期均線下彎呈現空頭排
列。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1100-1.1260 
前週交易區間 
1.1160 1.1276

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.05-115.10.08 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.66% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     2.05% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.82%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週邁入新的提存期 ， 央行連日減發存單 ， 令市場資金供
給充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週一央行標售 364 天期定存單 1100 億元 ， 加權平均得標利率為1.602%， 低於
市場預期區間 1.61%-1.635%，較上期標售結果下跌 4.1 個基本點。 
週 四 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 受美債利率續創新高影響，週四 10 年期指標券 A15109 成交在 2.05%，創同
年期券自 2008 年以來高點，5 年期指標券 A15108 則未成交，最近成交利率
為 1.82%。 
台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨
警戒線 ， 且創兩年半來最大漲幅 ， 目前油價仍居高難下 ， 通膨壓力將令央行維
持偏緊的貨幣政策，加上國際債市利率持續走升，台債殖利率恐面臨上行壓
力，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.95%，10 年券區間為 2.0%~2.10%。

### Page 1

Powertech Technology

Up-cycle level of earnings to continue; 
reiterate Buy

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 500.00 TWD | Price: 254.50 TWD

Reiterate Buy on above-trend growth and margins 
We believe Powertech Technology Inc. (PTI) can continue to benefit from the AI boom 
and an upturn in the memory chip industry. 3Q results clearly confirm record-high 
revenue (NT$25bn; up 27% YoY) and an above-trend gross margin (24%; normal high 
teen% or 20% range). 4Q guidance (for packaging/testing businesses) also remained 
bullish: DRAM – tight supply vs strong demand; NAND – order increase for more value-
added NAND and enterprise SSD; and logic – mix improvement with FC-BGA and price 
hike (following cost increase). Key customers are Micron, Kioxia, Solidigm (owned by SK 
Hynix) and various Greater China logic chip makers that require outsourced packaging 
and testing services. As we have already assumed up-cycle earnings level for 2027-28, 
our EPS estimates remain largely unchanged. We maintain our PO of NT$500 (27x 2027-
28E P/E applied). Reiterate Buy.  
Key takeaways from 3Q results  
Five takeaways from 3Q results: (1) strong execution in newly growing packaging orders 
for high-end memory (low-power / high-speed DRAM, enterprise SSD, 200+ layers 
NAND), (2) increasing exposure to more advanced logic (through FC-BGA, etc), (3) a well-
balanced sales mix (DRAM: 20% of total sales; NAND: 28%; SiP/modules 9%; logic 43%), 
(4) notably improved gross margins (limited increase in manufacturing costs vs strong 
top-line growth seen), and (5) solid FCF generation (EBITDA: c.NT$8bn vs capex: 
NT$5bn; expected annual dividend yield: 3-4% range).

Key Metrics      
* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 15.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Headquartered in Taiwan, PTI is one of the largest OSAT 
firms, which packages and tests memory and logic chips. It 
has around 18,000 employees. The logic chip-related 
business operates under Greatek Electronics (fully 
consolidated into earnings). Management's new focus areas 
in packaging/testing are PLP, TSV, 3D NAND, low-power 
DRAM, SSD, etc.

Investment Rationale 
Our Buy rating is based on strong earnings growth, driven 
by more value-added memory and even logic packaging 
solutions. 1Q-3Q26 earnings have already hit upcycle levels, 
posting a strong YoY growth (largely driven by NAND/SSD). 
We believe the memory super-cycle and customers' 
ongoing outsourcing trend (Micron, Solidigm/Hynix, Kioxia 
and Greater China's logic chipmakers) using Powertech's 
proven backend solutions should be the key contributors to 
strong earnings growth by 2028.

Stock Data  
Price to Book Value 3.0x

Broadcom), and customer wins such as AMD, positioning itself as a key beneficiary of growing AI semiconductor packaging demand from 2027 onward 
Powertech’s recent news summary during Jul-Aug 2026 
Date Key news Summary 
Aug-2026 FOPLP 
production 
-Powertech plans to start production at the world's first panel-level chip packaging facility for AI chips as soon as early 2027 to make server CPUs, AI 
computing chips and advanced optics 
-Powertech said its advanced FOPLP production line has secured a 100% UTR through 2030 ahead of its scheduled volume productio n in 2027 
-Powertech plans to invest NT$70bn (US$2.2bn) in its FOPLP facility, with installed capacity set to increase from 1,000 panels per months in 2026 
to 5,000 in 2028 
Jul-2026 JV with Broadcom -Powertech reported plans to invest US$400mn to create a new JV with Broadcom in Singapore to cater to customer demand for advanced panel-
based packaging services 
-The JV will concentrate on offering additive finer-geometry redistribution layer (RDL) tech for advanced package substrates  
 AMD business -Powertech reaffirmed that it is on track to start shipping AI chips utilizing its advanced PLP tech for AMD in 2H27, dismissing concern s over delays 
-AMD will be one of Powertech's first customers adopting the FOPLP tech, which is considered to be more cost-effective than the mainstream 
CoWoS tech 
  Capex/capacity -Powertech plans to acquire the entire Philippine unit of ON Semiconductor for US$45mn as part of its efforts to navigate global supply chain 
realignment amid escalating geopolitical risks 
-The transaction will be conducted by its subsidiary, Greatek Electronics in which Powertech holds 43% stake

### Page 13

Price objective basis & risk 
Powertech Technology (PWOGF) 
Our PO of NT$500 is derived from 27x P/E based on 2027-28E EPS. This is 10-20% 
higher than 2024-25 peak levels (low- to mid-20x). Since we expect 50%+ higher EPS in 
2026-28E vs 2024-25 on the back of record-high sales/margin, we believe fair multiples 
should be at least on par with the previous peak level. True, the long-term historical 
average P/E came in the range of 10-20x, but the memory super-cycle and AI boom 
should warrant above-trend earnings growth and consequently over 20x fair P/E. 
Taiwan's OSAT stocks also trade at 20-30x P/E.

Upside risk is mostly related to strong packaging orders from memory chipmakers and 
even logic chip suppliers.

Downside risk is mostly associated with an unexpected memory industry downturn or AI 
bubble (e.g., significant packaging order cut).

Update
Accton Technology Corporation  | Asia Pacific
Read-across from Celestica's
3Q26 Results
We see positive implications for Accton given demand strength
(1.6T migration amid sustaining 800G) and breadth (scale up
opportunities) of ethernet  switches in future.
Celestica (CLS.N, not covered) 3Q26 results:
• 3Q26 revenue of US$5,830mn (+24% QoQ/+83% YoY), non-GAAP operating
margin of 8.5%,  a record high, and non-GAAP EPS of US$3.30 (+30% QoQ/

+109% YoY).
° Higher than guidance and 7%/13% above consensus for revenue/earnings,
respectively.
° Drivers: Stronger CCS revenue (84% of the mix in 3Q26), including
networking and compute/storage growth of 68% and 242% YoY,
respectively, vs. guidance 60% and 190% YoY.
• 4Q26 revenue guided at US$6,275-6,575mn (+10% QoQ/+76% YoY at the
midpoint) vs. consensus at US$6,396mn, management raising 2026 target to
US$21bn for revenue (+69% YoY vs. prior US$20.5bn) and US$11.55 for non-

GAAP EPS  (+91% YoY vs. prior US$11.3).
° Driven by hyperscaler-led 800G demand and ramping 1.6T programs,
along with continued AI compute and storage demand.
• 2027 revenue guided at US$40bn (+90% YoY), non-GAAP OPM at 9.0%
(+0.6ppt YoY) and non-GAAP EPS at US$21.5 (+86% YoY)
° It expects CCS to grow 110% (1.6T/800G on networking and ASIC ramp
on compute/storage) and  ATS at 10%, with revenue skewed to  2H on
multiple large ramps and momentum likely sustaining into  2028.

• Pluggable should remain the mainstream interconnect solution, while CPO
might pick up more meaningfully from 3.2T, per company.
• CLS expects ethernet scale up could represent new opportunities in future.
Our view:
• While the customer profiles are different, we still see positive implications
from Celestica's comments for Accton regarding demand strength (1.6T
migration amid sustaining 800G) and breadth (scale up opportunities) of
ethernet  switches.

• We stay constructive on Accton, and believe   it will continue to benefit from
the secular growth in network switches amid increasing data traffic, spec
upgrades, and a wider customer base as well as the AI accelerator modules
on the initiatives for compute diversity.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Accton Technology Corporation (2345.TW)
Base case, P/E, which we believe better captures the company's earnings growth momentum.
We assign a target P/E of 30x to our 2027 EPS estimate, which we view as justified by an
operating profit CAGR of 48% for 2025-28, based on our estimates. This implies a PEG of
around 0.6, vs. its average of 0.7 over 2019-25, when the stock was trading at a P/E of 22x for
an operating profit CAGR of 32%.

Risks to Upside
n Stronger data center capex
n Faster network switch spec migration
n InfiniBand losing share in AI networks
n Stronger custom AI accelerator growth
n Stronger ODM direct business model adoption
Risks to Downside
n Weaker data center capex
n Slower network switch spec migration
n InfiniBand taking more share in AI networks
n Softer custom AI accelerator growth
n Softer ODM direct business model adoption

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

ASE Technology Holding (3711.TW)
29 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Stable traditional businesses and EMS outlook — Traditional packaging and test
demand is improving, supported by utilization gains and a recovery in automotive
and industrial, as well as AI-adjacent chips. Management expects general-market
business to grow again in 2027, despite softer smartphone and PC demand. For
2027, ASEH expects further EMS growth, led by high-performance computing, and

is pursuing system-level solutions that combine EMS capabilities with ATM
technologies.
LEAP upside intact; GM upward trend structural
Following the solid 3Q26 result, it expects 4Q26 consolidated revenue to grow 6–
8% QoQ, with gross margin of 23–24% and operating margin of 14.5–15.5%. ATM
revenue is forecast to increase 11–13% QoQ, with GM improving 100–200 bps. EMS
revenue is expected to be flat QoQ, with an operating margin of about 2%.
Looking into 2027, management expects LEAP revenue to double, with further GM

improvement as LEAP ramps and utilization rises across traditional packaging and
test. Demand remains ahead of available capacity: assembly is expected to grow
faster than test, with investment prioritizing assembly and wafer probing. Full-
process revenue is projected to exceed US$1bn in 2027, while CPO is expected to
contribute more than US$500m. Capacity and facility space remain key
constraints; ASE plans to increase capex to US$11.5bn in 2027 given its raised

2026 outlook. It also expects traditional-market growth to continue, supported by
automotive, industrial and AI-adjacent demand, despite softer smartphone and PC
markets.
On the back of its structural GM upside and more LEAP revenue contribution, we
lift our 2026/2027 /2028 earnings projection by 12%/18%/16%. We maintain Buy
with TP NT$908, based on an unchanged 25x 2027 EPS.

### Page 8

ASE Technology Holding (3711.TW)
29 October 2026 Citi Research
8
ASE Technology Holding
Company description
Advanced Semiconductor Engineering (ASE), incorporated in 1984 and listed
at the TWSE in 1989, is one of the world's leading companies in the
semiconductor packaging and testing sector. In June 2016, ASE entered into a
Joint Share Exchange Agreement with SPIL under which ASE Industrial was
eventually to be formed as a holding company with 100% equity interests in

both ASE and SPIL. ASE Industrial was listed on Taiwan's main board on April
30, 2018, the same day on which ASE and SPIL were delisted.
Investment strategy
We have a Buy rating on ASE Holding. The combined ASE-SPIL entity is the
highest-quality OSAT worldwide, in our view, with its market share
leadership, superior operations, economies of scale, and customer/product
diversification. Conditions imposed by the Chinese government on the ASE-
SPIL merger, which required them to operate independently, were lifted in

2020. Consequently, we believe ASE Holding will benefit from further revenue
and operating synergies as well as the trend for advanced packaging.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$908 is based on 25x 2027 EPS average. Our PER is
higher than its five-year range as we expect ASEH to see accelerating
earnings growth in the coming years with GM at upward expansion trend,
mainly driven by its promising outlook in LEAP business and strong expansion
for long-term AI demand. At our target price, the shares would trade at a

2026/27E P/B of 10x/8x.
Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent ASE shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) poor efficiency and less synergy created from merger with
SPIL; 2) failure to gain market share in advanced packaging; 3) industry
oversupply due to aggressive capacity expansion; 4) severe pricing erosion
due to competition; and 5) a longer-than-expected semiconductor inventory
correction.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

buy recommendation and a Catalyst Watch/STV Downside call corresponds to a sell recommendation. Any stock not assigned

## Document 132: 261029_daiwa_RTK

Source PDF: all report pdf/261029_daiwa_RTK.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: On 29 October, Realtek reported 3Q26 results that missed our estimates but 
were in line with the consensus. This was mainly due to weaker-than-expected revenue 
likely on a deeper demand rebalancing despite pricing power on supply-chain cost 
passthrough. While 4Q26 outlook looks intact, management expects steady topline growth 
into 2027, supported by a multi-year bandwidth upgrade cycle, continued pricing resilience 
and emerging AI opportunities from cloud to edge to physical AI, despite likely still subdued 
PC and consumer electronics demand. We rate the stock Outperform (2) premised upon 
our key thesis of a multi-year/multi-G (gigabits per second) bandwidth upgrade cycle 
across wireline and wireless to benefit Realtek, which is further echoed by management’s 
2027 outlook.

Highlight 
• 3Q26 results miss on lower topline. Realtek reported 3Q26 net profit of TWD4.1bn, 
missing our estimate by 7% while in line with the consensus. With financial metrics such 
as margins broadly in line, the miss was mainly due to lower-than-expected topline 
which came in 8% below our estimate at TWD37bn. We believe the weak revenue was 
driven by a deeper demand rebalancing after customer restocking and pre-stocking in 
1H26 on seasonality and supply-chain uncertainties, flagged in our note, Downgrading: 
pricing to bridge rebalancing but …, 4 August. Although Realtek raised prices to pass on 
cost inflation to the supply chain, it failed to offset such rebalancing. As usual, 
management gave no quantitative guidance for 4Q26 but expressing “prudent and 
steady” revenue run rates, in line with our expectations. We will be reviewing our model 
in due course.

• Key takeaways. At today’s results call, management further expected a steady topline 
growth heading into 2027, driven by: 1) multiple-G bandwidth upgrade cycles across 
wireline and wireless networking, including Wi-Fi 7 and 8, multi-G Ethernet, PON and 
switch, to enhance product mix with market-share gains adding to upside; 2) likely 
sustained pricing power with the company continuing to pass on inflated costs to the 
supply chain; and 3) despite likely still subdued demand from PC and consumer 
electronics markets, the ongoing development of AI, from cloud to edge AI to physical AI 
applications, is creating growth opportunities and tallying with our investment thesis of 
the multi-year/multi-G bandwidth upgrade cycle to structurally benefit Realtek.

Recommendation 
We rate Realtek Outperform (2). The stock price has generally consolidated around 
TWD670-790 over the past 3 months due to lack of catalysts amid its demand rebalancing, 
suggesting patience for investors. Key downside risk to our call would be any commodity 
price hikes leading to worse-than-expected demand rebalancing.

晟田(4541)：航太及半導體客戶需求延續，帶動 2027 年獲利成長 
建議：中立轉買進

一、晟田為航太精密機械零件加工廠 
晟田成立於 1991 年 8 月，實收資本額 6.76 億元，截至
2Q26 底，員工約 686 位。主要從事航太零組件(包括發
動機機匣與懸塊、起落架)及機械零組件(包括半導體真
空泵浦零組件、食品飲料填充包裝設備零組件、工業自
動化精密傳動零組件等產品)之製造加工與銷售等業務。
2025 年前五大客戶為漢翔(2634)、日本 E 客戶、
SLS(Safran Landing Systems)、SPP (6355.JP)及長榮
航太(2645)。2026 年前 9 個月產品別營收佔比為航太零
組件(客供料) 48%，航太零組件(自購料) 25%，半導體
設備零組件 23%，其他 4%。

二 、 夏季電價及費用認列影響獲利 ， 預估3Q26 稅後 EPS 
1.26 元 
晟田 3Q26 營收 5.6 億元(+1.9%QoQ，+30.9%YoY)，
季增主因為受惠航太客供料客戶拉貨動能成長及半導體
客戶需求提升。獲利方面，儘管產品組合持續優化，惟
受到夏季電價及籌資相關費用認列影響，預估營業利益
1.01 億元(-6%QoQ，+97.1%YoY)。假設匯率持穩，預
估稅後淨利 0.85 億元(-1%QoQ，+34.4%YoY)， 稅後EPS 
1.26 元。 
三、產品組合持續優化，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 1.3 元 
展望 4Q26，半導體方面，考量客戶需求持續強勁，有
望帶動出貨動能；航太方面，波音(BA.US) 737 系列機
型月產能有望於 2026 年底自 42 架提升至 47 架，另，
根據空中巴士(EADSY.US)規劃，2026 年預計交付 870
架，截至 Sep-26 交付達成率約 6 成，預期 4Q26 將加
速交機 ， 帶動航太零組件拉貨需求 。 整體而言 ， 預估4Q26
營收 5.62 億元(+0.5%QoQ，+20.7%YoY)。獲利方面，
隨產品組合持續優化，且無電價因素干擾，預估營業利
益 1.05 億元(+3.9%QoQ，+28.6%YoY)。 假設匯率持穩，
預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 1.3 元。 
四、需求延續，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 4.51 元 
展望後市，儘管航太供應鏈仍受原物料短缺、人力及產
能瓶頸影響，惟波音及空巴在手訂單仍維持高位，顯示
終端需求具支撐。隨供應鏈瓶頸逐步緩解，預期將帶動
航太零組件拉貨需求回升，有利晟田營運成長。半導體
方面，受惠終端客戶持續擴大資本支出，帶動相關產品
需求穩定成長。整體而言，預估 2026 年營收 21.2 億元
(+29.1%YoY)。獲利方面，隨產品組合持續優化，預估
毛利率 25.1%，稅後淨利 3.1 億元(+122.9%YoY)，稅後
EPS 4.51 元。 
展望 2027 年，考量航空引擎零組件客戶需求強勁，晟
田規劃擴充相關產能，新廠有望於 2H27 投產；半導體
方面，持續優化相關產能，以因應客戶需求成長。整體
而言，預估 2027 年營收 23.5 億元(+10.7%YoY)。獲利
方面，考量新廠投產後折舊費用增加，預估毛利率年減
至 24.8%， 稅後淨利3.3 億元(+9%YoY)， 稅後EPS 4.58
元(以增資稀釋後股本計算)。 
五、中立轉買進理由 
評價方面，以 2027 年 EPS 估算，目前 PE 約為 13X，
介於晟田歷史區間 12X~20X 下緣 ， 調升至買進評等 ， 目
標價 75 元(2026F PE 17X)。

## Document 178: 9月工业企业利润数据点评：价格结构性抬升下行业利润分化或加剧

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月工业企业利润数据点评：价格结构性抬升下行业利润分化或加剧.pdf

读懂 8 月工业企业盈利的结构性修复 ，
2026 年9 月28 日

如何看待利润增长放缓下的行业“温差”？
2026 年8 月27 日

企业利润延续两位数增速，下半年分化仍
是主要特征，2026 年7 月27 日

价格与利润率支撑工业企业利润高增，电
子行业贡献超四成，2026 年6 月29 日

工业企业利润延续改善，上下游分化进一
步加剧，2026 年5 月27 日

价格上涨支撑工业企业利润延续改善，原
材料和新动能表现亮眼，2026 年4 月27 日

价格结构性抬升下行业利润分化或加剧 
             ——9 月工业企业利润数据点评

事件：1-9 月工业企业营收同比增长 6.6%，较去年同期提升
4.2 个百分点，与今年 1-8 月持平；1-9 月工业企业利润同比增长
15.5%，较去年同期提升12.3 个百分点、较今年1-8 月下降0.2 个
百分点，其中 9 月当月利润同比增长 14.4%，较去年同期下降 7.2
个百分点，较今年8 月提升10.2 个百分点。 
量价同时改善，营收利润率小幅抬升，共同支撑 9 月利润增速
边际回升。1-9 月工业企业利润同比增长 15.5%，其中 9 月工业企
业利润同比增长 14.4%，较上月回升 10.2 个百分点。营收方面，
1-9 月工业企业营收同比增长6.6%，与1-8 月持平。其中，量的方
面，外需强劲支撑工业生产，9 月出口交货值同比增长 17%，较上
月提升 5.9 个百分点，带动 9 月工业增加值同比增长 5.5%，较上
月提升 0.3 个百分点。价的方面，受中东局势紧张、AI 产业快速
发展、新能源相关需求抬升等因素影响，国际原油、有色等大宗
商品价格上行，带动9 月PPI 同比增长5%，较上月大幅提升1.2 个
百分点。营收利润率方面，1-9 月营业收入利润率为 5.68%，我们
测算 9 月单月营业收入利润率为 5.81%、较上月上升 0.14 个百分
点，结合成本费用来看，9 月单月成本率较上月下降 0.89 个百分
点至84.65%、费用率较上月上升0.4 个百分点至8.35%，整体来看
9 月企业盈利能力较上月稍有改善。
工业企业利润数据点评 2026 年10 月27 日   2026 年第59 期

9 月工业企业利润数据点评： 
价格结构性抬升下行业利润分化或加剧 
企业产成品库存与上月基本持平，应收账款回收期略有缩短 。9 月工业企业产成品存
货同比增长 11.6%，较上月上升 0.6 个百分点，从金额来看，9 月产成品存款 7.37 万亿，
与上月基本持平，但与9 月制造业PMI 数据背离，9 月制造业PMI 产成品库存分项为47.6%、
较上月下降 0.8 个百分点，可能因为制造业 PMI 产成品库存反映的是产成品数量的变化，
而工业企业产成品存货反映的是金额的变化，二者背离说明产成品存货单位成本的上升，
与近期 PPI 走强表现一致。9 月工业企业应收账款余额同比增长 10%，较 1-8 月上升 1 个
百分点，而应收账款回收期较 8 月下滑 0.6 天至 71.6 天，主要是因为 9 月营业收入环比
增速（7.89%）高于应收账款余额环比增速（0.97%），表明 9 月工业企业现销收入增加，
以及应收账款回收力度加大，与9 月企业活期存款增加相一致。 
国有企业利润增速边际抬升，私营企业利润增速边际下滑。1-9 月国企利润同比增长
13.1%，较 1-8 月上升 2.8 个百分点，原油等大宗商品价格上行或是主要原因；私营企业
利润同比增长 8.7%，较 1-8 月下降 1.7 个百分点，今年整体呈“前高后低”态势；股份
制企业利润同比增长20.8%，较1-8 月小幅下降0.4 个百分点；高基数下外商及港澳台企
业利润同比增长0.5%，较1-8 月下降 1.8 个百分点。 
图1：9 月工业企业利润增速回升 图2：量、价对工业企业利润增长形成支撑

2 
www.ccxi.com.cn 
9 月工业企业利润数据点评： 
价格结构性抬升下行业利润分化或加剧 
行业利润分化加剧， 超六成行业利润负增长， 原油与有色相关产业链和电子产业链
利润持续高增，消费品制造业盈利修复仍需时日。1-9 月采矿业利润同比增长 38.2%，较
1-8 月抬升 3.1 个百分点，利润延续高增。国际原油价格高位运行，以及 AI、新能源相关
产业链对有色金属的需求旺盛是主要原因，1-9 月，石油开采和有色金属采选业利润分别
同比增长 21.3%和 68%。1-9 月制造业利润同比增长 17%，较 1-8 月微降0.4 个百分点，原
材料制造业和高技术制造业表现亮眼，1-9 月，原材料制造业利润同比增长 44.9%，其中，
原油相关的化工、化纤制造行业利润分别同比增长 49.2%和 81.7%，有色金属加工业利润
同比增长 79.7%；高技术制造业利润同比增长 52%，其中电子行业利润同比增长 1.0 倍，
拉动全部规模以上工业企业利润增长 9.6 个百分点。但是，终端消费需求疲弱下，消费制
造业利润持续负增长，1-9 月，汽车、农副食品加工、纺织服装、家具制造等行业利润分
别同比下滑 22.8%、17.3%、15.6%和 51.3%。1-9 月公共事业利润同比下滑 14.3%，主要源
于电力、热力生产和供应业利润同比下滑 17.9%，今年以来跌幅持续扩大，煤炭价格上行
抬升生产成本，电力市场化改革推动电价下降，共同挤压电力行业利润。 
与去年同期相比，中游行业利润占比相对稳定，上游、下游行业利润占比一升一降。
1-9 月，上、中、下游行业利润占比
1
分别为 26.26%、50.8%和 22.94%，与上月相比变化不
大，但与去年同期相比，上游、下游行业利润占比分别上升 5.45 和下降 8.18 个百分点，
反映原材料价格上涨以及终端需求疲弱对企业利润的挤压。中游行业利润占比变化不大，
内部出现一定结构性分化，其中，电子产业利润占比较去年同期上升 7.56 个百分点，化

1
 上中下游行业口径较之前有所调整，同时不再计算公共事业行业利润占比。

9 月工业企业利润数据点评： 
价格结构性抬升下行业利润分化或加剧 
工、化纤制造行业利润占比也有小幅提升，而其余行业如金属 /非金属制造、通用/专用/
运输设备制造等行业利润占比则较去年同期有不同程度下降。 
整体来看，在工业生产加快、价格上行带动下，9 月工业企业利润回升至两位数增速。
展望后续，四季度行业利润 或延续改善趋势，但 分化延续，利润仍将向原油、有色金属
以及 AI 相关产业集中。其一，中东冲突局势未定，近来国际原油价格持续维持在 100 美
元/桶的高位，利好石油开采及其相关的化工化纤行业利润增长，同时，冬季采暖叠加对
原油的替代需求增加，煤炭行业利润也将延续亮眼表现。其二，AI、新能源产业快速发展，
对铜、铝、锂等有色金属的需求旺盛，支撑有色金属矿采和加工行业利润。其三， AI 投
资热度未减，相关产品价格上涨显著，需求的快速增长带动先进存储、电子电路制造等电
子行业利润快速增长，同时，算力与数据中心建设带动光纤制造、光缆制造等行业利润抬
图5：1-9 月采矿业、制造业利润保持高增 图6：1-9 月利润向上游集中

## Document 211: [034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡는다면 지금두산에너빌리티(034020) 3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡는다면 지금3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡_62bfa2bd

Source PDF: all report pdf/[034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡는다면 지금두산에너빌리티(034020) 3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡는다면 지금3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡_62bfa2bd.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

Company Brief 
유틸리티/건설 
Analyst 문경원, CFA 
kyeongwon.moon@meritz.co.kr 
RA 이동석 
dongseok.lee@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 Review: 바닥을 잡는다면 지금  
믹스 개선에 따른 이익률 개선 조금씩 관찰 중 
3Q26 연결 영업이익은 2,612억원(+90.6% YoY) 을 기록하며 컨센서스(2,455억원)
를 소폭 상회했다. 밥캣(1,674억원)이 컨센서스(1,761억원)를 소폭 하회한 가운데, 
에너빌리티 부문 영업이익(+179% YoY, OPM 6.6%) 이 호실적을 이끌었다. 환율 하
락에도 불구, 가스터빈, 원전 등 고수익성 프로젝트의 비중 상승이 주요 원인이다. 
3Q26 누적 수주는 10.0조원(+85.5% YoY) 을 기록했다. 4Q26 가스복합 EPC 수주 
등을 감안하면 연간 가이던스(13.3조원) 달성은 무난하다. 한수원과의 협상 결렬로 
이목이 집중된 체코 두코바니 시공 수주는 2027년 인식될 확률이 높아 보인다. 주간
사가 바뀌더라도 이미 프로젝트에 참여 중인 동사를 배제하기는 쉽지 않다.

대미투자 이후, 연말~연초 수주 모멘텀에 집중 
연말 연초에 다음과 수주 모멘텀이 집중되면서, 주가 상승을 이끌 전망이다. 
1) 대형원전: 정부 자금 중심의 Westinghouse LLI(장납기 기자재) 발주가 몰린다. 
우선 미국 DOE(에너지부)가 제공한 대출을 기반으로 미국 수주가 기대된다.  폴란드 
정부 역시 최근 LLI 발주 작업을 시작했다. 한편, 한국 정부는 연말까지 100억 달러 
규모의 대미 투자 송금을 협의 중인데, 이 역시 DOC(상무부) 주도의 대형 원전 건설
을 위한 LLI 발주에 쓰일 가능성이 높다 
2) SMR: 오래 기다려왔던 Nuscale 향 수주가 시작되고 있다. 우선 29일 모듈 1기 
제작 계약을 체결했다. 현금이 부족한 Nuscale이 용기를 냈다는 건, 곧 Nuscale-
TVA 간 PPA 가 임박했음을 의미한다. 본격적인 수주는 PPA 직후 예상된다.  
3) 가스 터빈: 2026년 초 해외 수주(7기)는 2029년까지 인도 계획이다. 이후 2030
년 슬랏 예약을 완료하고 2031년 물량을 논의 중으로 보인다. 지금은 2030년 슬랏
에 대한 PO가 나와 수주잔고에 인식할 타이밍이다. 역시 수출 물량으로 추측한다.

성장에 대한 기대가 사라진 밸류에이션, 지금이 바닥 
투자의견 Buy를 유지, 할인율 상승을 반영하여 적정주가를 109,000원으로 -17% 
하향 조정한다. 금리 상승, 환율 하락, 트럼프 지지율 하락 등 Top-down 리스크에 
최근 조정을 받았지만, 추가적인 주가 하락 폭은 제한적일 전망이다. EV/선행 수주잔
고 지표는 현재 1.0배 수준까지 하락하며, 원전의 성장성이 주목받지 못했던 
2021~2024년 수준에 근접하게 내려왔다.  
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 30

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 109,000원 
현재주가 (10.29) 56,700원 
상승여력 92.2%

### Page 2

2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -2.62 điểm tương ứng -0.15%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Công nghệ và Máy móc là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -13,987 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã HDB, PNJ và VJC; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-30 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,267 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -2,363 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 3,726 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -2,702 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua một tuần đi ngang giằng co, giảm nhẹ và đóng cửa ở 1.735 điểm 
với thanh khoản ở mức thấp, và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng cả 5 phiên trong tuần. 
Thông tin vĩ mô tích cực với tăng trưởng GDP quý 3 đạt mức gần 10% là điểm sáng trong 
tuần qua, tuy đã không đủ nâng đỡ thị trường chung. Thay vào đó, tâm lý thị trường thận 
trọng trước số liệu lạm phát ở mức cao, đợt tăng giá xăng dầu trong nước và mặt bằng lãi 
suất trong nước chưa có dấu hiệu hạ nhiệt. Diễn biến tăng của giá dầu thế giới, và lợi suất 
trái phiếu Chính phủ Mỹ cũng là các yếu tố gây áp lực lên thị trường chung. Tâm lý thận 
trọng còn thể hiện qua áp lực bán giải chấp ở một số cổ phiếu riêng lẻ như PNJ, SHB..., dù 
một vài nhóm trụ như dầu khí, tiêu dùng có thời điểm đóng vai trò nâng đỡ chỉ số.

### Page 3

Trần Đức Anh 
Giám đốc Kinh tế Vĩ mô & Chiến lược thị trường 
anhtd@kbsec.com.vn 
07/10/2026

Triển vọng TTCK 4Q2026 
Tích lũy trong vùng nhiễu động

Chúng tôi dự báo vùng điểm hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm 2026 
xuống 1,800 đi ểm, giảm mạnh từ mức 1,950 đi ểm đưa ra trong báo cáo gần nhất. 
Vùng điểm này tương ứng:

- Tăng trưởng EPS 2026 so với cùng kỳ của thị trường chung ở mức 33% 
(tăng mạnh so với mức 25% của báo cáo gần nhất), chủ yếu nhờ kỳ vọng 
vào các chính sách thúc đẩy kinh tế của Chính phủ, và lợi nhuận tăng 
trưởng đột biến ở VIC (xem thêm phần Triển vọng hoạt động kinh doanh).

- Mức P/E hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm ở mức 13.2 (giảm 
nhẹ so với mức 13.5 thời điểm hiện tại - phản ánh quan điểm các yếu tố 
rủi ro sẽ tiếp tục duy trì). Đây là mức định giá thấp so với bình quân 5 năm 
gần nhất ở 15.1. Nếu loại bỏ yếu tố đột biến ở nhóm Vingroup, chỉ số P/E 
điều chỉnh hiện đã rơi xuống 10.9, thấp hơn các đáy gần nhất năm 2020, 
2022 và 2025. Trong bối cảnh kinh tế Việt Nam duy trì tốc độ tăng trưởng 
cao, lợi nhuận các doanh nghiệp niêm yết liên tục bứt phá, vùng định giá 
này mở ra cơ hội đầu tư trung/dài hạn. Chúng tôi cho rằng ngay khi các rủi 
ro vĩ mô quốc tế (xoay quanh vấn đề Iran), và trong nước (xoay quanh vấn 
đề lãi suất) hạ nhiệt, thị trường chung sẽ có cơ hội tái định giá lên mức cao 
hơn đáng kể so với thời điểm hiện tại. Dù vậy, khác với báo cáo gần nhất 
vào tháng 7, ở báo cáo này, chúng tôi đẩy lui thời điểm các yếu tố rủi ro vĩ 
mô hạ nhiệt sang năm 2027, đồng nghĩa với quan điểm thận trọng hơn về 
thị trường cho quý 4 2026.

Cụ thể, đối với vấn đề lãi suất, áp lực lạm phát lớn dần, đi kèm nhu cầu vốn cao phục 
vụ hoạt động đầu tư cơ sở hạ tầng sẽ khiến mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức cao cho 
phần còn lại của năm 2026 (dù thanh khoản hệ thống ghi nhận sự cải thiện đáng kể 
trong 1 quý gần đây). Đối với vấn đề xung đột Trung Đông, các cuộc đàm phán vẫn 
đang bế tắc và ít có kh ả năng dòng ch ảy dầu mỏ tại khu vực này được nối lại ngay 
trong năm nay.

Chủ đề đầu tư chúng tôi l ựa chọn cho 4Q2026 là đ ầu tư công, chính sách ti ền tệ hỗ 
trợ, và xung đột Iran.

Xem đầy đủ tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành mẫu nến Doji trên khung đồ thị tuần sau hai tuần giảm điểm liên tiếp, 
cho thấy áp lực bán đã phần nào được kiểm soát. Trên khung ngày, nỗ lực hồi phục đầu 
tuần gặp lực cản tại đường SMA50 khiến VNINDEX quay đầu giảm điểm, tiếp tục chớm phá 
đáy ngắn hạn; tuy nhiên vùng hỗ trợ gần đã tạo hiệu ứng thu hút lực cầu bắt đáy, giúp chỉ 
số thu hẹp đà giảm trong phiên cuối tuần. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh khá tiêu cực từ vùng đỉnh 
đầu tháng 9, VNINDEX đang bước vào giai đoạn tìm kiếm điểm cân bằng trở lại và diễn biến 
giằng co điều chỉnh có thể còn kéo dài thêm trong các phiên kế tiếp. Tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn tính từ vùng đáy cuối tháng 7 vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại tại điểm đỡ gần quanh 1710 (đây cũng là chốt chặn cần 
giữ để bảo vệ được xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn).

## Document 314: Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Jan Hatzius 
+1(212)902-0394 | jan.hatzius@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP .  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

### Page 2

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

### Page 4

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

6. The Fed’s recent signals—explicit from Williams/Jefferson and implicit from Warsh—
effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

another hike in March, but either way we see renewed cuts in late 2027/early 2028.

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.

November 5.  Consistent with our view that the UK economy is stronger than widely 
perceived, GDP growth has surprised on the upside this year and the unemployment rate 
declined to 4.7% in August on a 3-month average basis (with the volatile 1-month rate 
plunging to 4.1%).  Rising inflation and the looming household energy price reset in 
January imply a significant risk of further moves, although market pricing of 4+ hikes 
(including November) still looks excessive.  We also expect hikes in Canada and New 
Zealand this week and in Australia next week.

9. Slightly faster than expected growth in Q3 has pushed up our 2026 GDP forecast for 
China to 4.6%.  But most activity indicators are much weaker.  Both property activity and 
fixed asset investment are contracting year-on-year, and while nominal retail sales 
growth picked up to +1.1% in September, large retailers (whose sales are relatively 
better measured) are still posting substantial year-on-year declines.  With domestic 
demand rising slowly at best, sustained GDP growth in the official 4.5-5% target range 
will require ongoing steep increases in the trade surplus.  But the surplus is already at 1% 
of global GDP , and further increases will eat more noticeably into output and 
employment abroad.  Ultimately, China’s trading partners may respond by imposing 
trade barriers that weigh on Chinese exports and GDP , although the measures taken so 
far have been limited.

10. Our strongest global view is that central banks will deliver fewer hikes than currently 
priced.  In addition, our rates strategists flag underweight positioning, diminished 
technical risks, and risk asset pressures as reasons why the recent rally might extend.  
The market’s narrow breadth makes our equity strategists wary of momentum factor 
exposure, but they still expect the S&P 500 to resume its climb after the midterm 
election, as earnings continue to grow solidly.  Our credit strategists remain cautious on 
IG technology bonds but see scope for some near-term reversal in HY given the scale of 
the recent selloff.  Our FX strategists expect the Japanese yen to act as a hedge against 
further AI jitters, in part because such jitters would shrink US-Japan interest rate 
differentials.  And our commodity strategists recommend targeted exposures to oil 
products and European natural gas to hedge near-term escalation risks alongside 
longer-term exposures to copper, gold, and select power markets to benefit from 
structural supply/demand imbalances.

1

SPECIAL EDITION | HOW BAS IS THE RISE 
IN BOND YIELDS?

TRANSCRIPTION

0:00:10 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Everyone, welcome. As we head into autumn 2026, interest rates are reaching levels never seen for many, many years. 
Why is this happening? Is it sustainable? And what are the implications for the economies of different countries? All these 
questions will be discussed over the next few minutes with the economists of BNP Paribas. Let us begin with Isabelle 
Mateos y Lago, chief economist of the group. Hello, Isabelle.  
0:00:38 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everyone. 
0:00:39 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you for being with us today. Just to begin, a simple explanation. What rates are we talking about and which countries 
are affected? 
0:00:48 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, it is very important to clarify this. So, we are talking about long-term interest rates, rates at 10 years or longer 
maturities, 30 years, etc. The rates at which sovereign states borrow on the market. And this is primarily something that is 
happening in advanced countries, so United States, United King dom, Japan, and then the Eurozone countries. And indeed, 
as you said, we are seeing since the beginning of the summer, interest rates reach levels we had not seen for about 20 
years. But it is important to keep in mind that up until that point, up until the mid -2000s, the level of interest rates that 
we're seeing now were really quite common, quite normal, so one shouldn't overdramatize.  
0:01:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why do we find ourselves in this situation and how did we get to this point?  
0:01:41 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, so the direct cause is that investors are demanding higher interest rates in order to  lend their capital to governments. 
But why is this? Well, there's essentially three reasons for this, and not all of them are bad, and they're not mutually 
exclusive either. The top reason to my mind is that there's just greater competition for capital be tween sovereign states 
and the corporate sector, who are all facing simultaneously additional investment needs, in defense, in artificial 
intelligence, in energy transition, in resilience. And so,  you just have greater competition for capital and that pushes up the 
price of capital, which is the interest rate. 
Reason number two is because markets have revised upwards their expectations of how high central banks are going to be 
taking their interest rates. 
And this mechanically translates into higher rates at the long end. And then there's a third reason, which doesn't apply 
necessarily in every case, but which is essentially higher sovereign risk, a kind of a risk premium, in cases where there are  
concerns arising about the sustainability of these public debts.

### Page 2

2

0:02:59 - Emmanuel Laborde 
What are the implications for global economies? 
0:03:03 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
So that's exactly what we'll be discussing in the next segments. But basically, the two key issues are, one, an adverse 
impact on public finances. It's higher interest rates making it harder to balance the books in terms of public accounts. And 
then secondly, these higher rates tend to slow down economic growth. Of course, by how much and how serious it will be, 
depends on how high interest rates go and how long they stay there. But that's for the next segments.  
0:03:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Yes. Thank you very much, Isabelle. Having said the scene now, let's move on to the first segment with the following 
question. What makes debt sustainable? And let's see what Hélène Baudchon has to say.  
Hello Hélène and welcome into this special edition. 
0:03:58 - Hélène Baudchon 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everybody. 
0:04:01 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When economists talk about public debt, they often refer to the sustainability of debt. As an economist, can you explain to 
us what that means and what is the debt trajectory deemed unsustainable?  
0:04:15 - Hélène Baudchon 
To begin with, I will say that a temporary increase in debt can be seen as a normal evolution, not particularly concerning, 
for instance during an economic crisis or more recently during a health crisis. In such cases, it acts as a buffer. A problem  
arises when the debt level never comes back down. An unsustainable trajectory can be therefore defined, characterized by 
a continuous regular rise in the debt level, both in absolute terms and as a proportion of GDP, driven by high and recurring 
fiscal deficits. And sometimes interest payments can exacerbate the problem.  
0:05:00 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In very simple terms, you are talking about the snowball effect.  
0:05:03 - Hélène Baudchon 
Exactly explaining why consolidation measures are needed in order to prevent such an adverse situation from occurring 
and in order to prevent, in fact, a sudden loss of confidence from creditors.  
0:05:16 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Regarding governments, what should serve as a warning sign for them? And at what point could the situation become, let's 
say, problematic? 
0:05:27 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, simply speaking, when interest rates rise, not so long ago, up until 2022, Eurozone countries were borrowing at very 
low rates and in some instance, even negative rates. And although debt levels were already at high level at that time, the 
low rates helped manage debt servicing. Not much fiscal effort was needed to keep the public debt ratios in check.  
0:05:55 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Of course, we all remember this because it was a very unusual situation, but now things are very different.

### Page 3

3

0:06:01 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed, the situation today is clearly more challenging simply because bond yields are significantly higher and they 
are rising at the time of our discussion. This is for the part the result of the ECB's monetary tightening in 2022 -2023 aimed 
at addressing the inflation shock then. Then long-term interest rates have risen as a consequence and have aligned more 
closely with GDP growth rate. And in technical terms, this is what we refer to as a narrowing of the "r" - "g" spread. 
0:06:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why is this spread so important? Why do you, the economists, monitor it this way?  
0:06:48 - Hélène Baudchon 
Essentially, you have the interest rate "r", which determines the debt burden, while the growth rate "g" determines the 
trajectory of fiscal revenues. And if "r" is lower than "g", and this has been the case for many years, as you can see on the  
chart on the screen, it means that interest payments are increasing at a slower pace than the revenue component of the 
equation. 
0:07:14 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this provides some leeway in other budgetary areas, such as education, of course, defense, maybe pensions.  
0:07:22 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed. And this is what we refer to as the primary budget. However, if and when interest rates do go up while the 
economy isn't growing that much faster, then fiscal leeway diminishes.  
0:07:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Crystal clear. Now, just could you provide a few last words to wrap up the sustainability segment?  
0:07:43 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, to conclude, we are entering a period which demands greater fiscal discipline or, as some might say, less indiscipline 
in order to manage debt trajectories effectively, in particular in the countries which have high debt levels. And for instanc e, 
the Italian case is an interesting one because it is well known for its high debt level ratio. But in fact, it also has an 
advantageous position because it has no primary deficit. In fact, it even benefits from a large primary surplus.  
0:08:22 - Emmanuel Laborde 
As for the countries... For the other countries... I stop you because this will be a discussion with Guillaume Derrien during  
the next segment. Thank you very much, Hélène. 
0:08:30 - Hélène Baudchon 
Thank you, Emmanuel. 
0:08:36 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Guillaume. Welcome. 
0:08:37 - Guillaume Derrien 
Thank you. 
0:08:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Emmanuel, and hello to everyone. The flexibility of fiscal policy depends heavily on interest rates, which have 
increased considerably over the past few months. The question is, where do we stand today?

### Page 4

4

0:08:52 - Guillaume Derrien 
So, long-term rates have increased in all advanced economies, reaching levels not seen since 2008 in Europe and in the 
United States, and even since 1996 in Japan. Fortunately, the effective rate that states pay on their debt is lower than thos e 
headline figures suggest, simply because the average maturity of debt is relatively long. Precisely how long are we talking 
about? So, it's about 10 years in Japan, 13 years in the United Kingdom, and 8.5 years in France. That means that most of 
the debt was raised before rates started climbing. 
For example, in France, the effective rate just recently rose above the 2% mark, which is significantly lower than the 10 -
year bond yield, which exceeded 4% for new issuances.  
That said, in the United States, which has an average maturity of roughly six years, is experiencing a more rapid increase in  
its effective rate. 
0:09:54 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this rate increase is happening in the context of high debt -to-GDP ratios and with modest growth, correct? Yes, that's 
correct. 
0:10:05 - Guillaume Derrien 
Debt levels have reached unprecedented heights since the end of World War II. And at the same time, growth is lower, 
hovering around 2% on average in the United States, 1% in Europe and 0.5% in Japan. What does that mean for debt 
servicing costs? So, debt servicing cost is already relatively high, but it is expected to grow further as debt issue before the 
2022 rate hikes mature and are replaced with new instruments bearing higher interest rates. By 2030, interest costs could 
rise to levels last seen in 2000, specifically in France, around 4% of GDP. 
Are all the countries equally affected by these trends? Actually, no. Some countries are managing to reduce their debt 
level. 
Spain, Italy, even Japan have sufficiently improved their primary balance to achieve this, at least for the time being. And o f 
course, we must speak about Germany. Yes, Germany is implementing investment programs that result in a higher public 
deficit and debt-to-GDP ratio. But this ratio started from a relatively low base at around above 60% of GDP. That said, in 
this current context, German bonds have risen. And because German bonds are used as a benchmark for Europe, it  raises 
rates across all Eurozone countries. Is France the only country seeing its public debt go up?  
No, it's not, Emmanuel. This is happening in the United Kingdom and in the United States. These economies, like France, 
are running public deficits that are ever close or exceed 5% of GDP, which is too large for debt ratio to stabilize on its ow n. 
France and the United Kingdom are trying to gradually reduce their public deficit so as not to compromise growth. The 
United States is not going on that path yet, but market pressure could eventually force them to do so.  
0:12:08 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much for your input, Guillaume. Thank you, Emmanuel. Let's go to the next segment. It's about the impact 
of rising bond yields on financing and investments in the Eurozone.  
0:12:26 - Thomas Humblot 
Hello, Thomas. Welcome. Hello. Thank you for having me today.  
0:12:30 - Emmanuel Laborde 
We've just seen what's behind the rise in Eurozone bond yields. This rise is partly attributed to the sovereign spread. What 
impact will this have on the economy? 
0:12:42 - Thomas Humblot 
Actually, this widening of some government bond spreads is obviously not good news, but it tends paradoxically to have 
less impact than an increase in long-term interest rates that does not include the sovereign risk premium. for example, in 
France, for a very long time, it has been considered that the 10 -year OAT was the relevant benchmark for fixed loans. And 
at that time, the spread on French government bonds was relatively stable. But since 2024, that spread increased

### Page 5

5

significantly and that renders the sovereign yield a less relevant benchmark.  
And now the most relevant benchmark is the swap rate because it determines the cost of bank funding that backs new 
loans. 
0:13:56 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Since the end of 2024, we have seen a rise in long-term 10-year swap rates. What impact does this have on bank lending 
rates? 
0:14:08 - Thomas Humblot 
Fixed loans have obviously become more expensive, but to a lesser extent than if they were priced solely based on long -
term sovereign rates. And what about existing fixed-rate loans? 
There is no impact. An increase in rates for the entire maturity of a loan does not have any effect if it is a fixed -rate loan. 
Borrowers with outstanding loans are therefore protected. Obviously, new fixed loans are granted at higher rates. And this 
sharply contrasts with a floating-rates loans. 
Because with floating-rates loans, borrowers experience regular rate adjustments throughout the term of the loan. And in 
terms of bank financing? Strictly in terms of bank financing, an increase in short -term rates has an immediate and 
widespread restrictive impact on borrowers, while for long-term interest rates, the impact is more gradual, and it comes 
gradually as the outstanding amount of fixed-rate loans are progressively renewed. And the thing is that since the 
beginning of the war in Iran, we have seen a combination of the two.  
And in practical terms, the rate of new bank loans to non-financial corporations has increased about 25 basis points in the 
eurozone since the beginning of the year. 
0:16:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Just a few words about companies that borrow directly from the market.  
0:16:16 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, in that case, the impact is even more straightforward. The rate at which a company borrows from the market is the 
risk-free rate plus a premium. 
0:16:28 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Have risk premiums, known as corporate credit spreads, also increased?  
0:16:34 - Thomas Humblot 
Yes, they have increased temporarily at the beginning of the year but have returned to normal since. For the time being, the 
widening remains moderate for the highest-rated investment-grade issuers. 
However, some significant bond issuance in the euro debt market due to some major US technology companies has 
increased spreads for longer maturities. 
0:17:11 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In particular terms, how does this affect the investment decisions of the companies?  
0:17:19 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, investments that were previously worthwhile due to returns sufficient to cover the cost of capital may no longer do so 
and in turn that might lead to a decrease in demand for financing.  
0:17:40 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much Thomas. Now let us move on to the emerging economies. And it is with Cynthia Kalasopatan -Antoine.

### Page 6

6

Hello Cynthia, and welcome. 
0:17:54 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Hi, Emmanuel. Hello, everyone. 
0:17:57 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Long-term bond yields rose significantly in advanced economies over the summer. What can we now say about emerging 
markets? 
0:18:06 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Emerging bond markets held up well, which is quite unusual. The rise in long -term sovereign yields in emerging countries 
has been less pronounced than in advanced economies, including the United States and Germany. Now, there are two 
main exceptions, Turkey and Poland. Meanwhile, we did not witness any significant correction in emerging financial 
markets over the summer, and the main currencies appreciated slightly against the dollar or the euro.  
0:18:37 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Does this mean that public finances are more robust in emerging countries?  
0:18:43 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, it depends. You see, in cases like the Czech Republic and South Korea, public debt is moderate and macroeconomic 
fundamentals are solid. However, these countries are now less frequently considered as emerging and tend to be viewed 
as newly advanced economies. By contrast, Brazil and South Africa have weaker public finances and investors have 
already incorporated a higher sovereign risk into their assessments. And this is reflected in the bond yields. And this 
assessment of sovereign risk did not really change over the summer. 
0:19:26 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When comparing the main fiscal metrics, what are the trends? What do you see as an economist?  
0:19:33 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
On average, the fiscal metrics in emerging markets are not substantially worse than those in advanced economies. The 
average budget deficit levels are similar, ranging from 4% to 5% of GDP in 2025 -2026, according to the IMF. And as in 
advanced economies, the public debt is higher than its pre-Covid level. However, the average public debt to GDP ratio in 
emerging countries excluding China is close to 60%, whereas in advanced economies, it is above 100%.  
0:20:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Do you arrive at the same conclusion when comparing debt sustainability criteria?  
0:20:17 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, there are significant regional differences. In emerging Asia, the factors that determine debt sustainability are more 
often favorable than those in advanced economies. In Central Europe, the gap between the interest rate paid on debt and 
the GDP growth rate is also favorable. However, the primary deficits in some countries are high.  
0:20:45 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In a few words, a brief overview of the situation in Latin America.  
0:20:49 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, Brazil and Mexico are in a less favorable position. These countries would indeed need to generate a significant 
primary budget surplus each year in order to stabilize their debt in the medium term.

### Page 7

7

0:21:07 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Economists use the term original sin, which refers to the situation when governments resort to borrowing in foreign 
currency to address the shortfall in domestic financing. Does this phenomenon continue to distinguish emerging 
countries? 
0:21:24 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Yes, this is still the case. Emerging countries still have a significant share of their debt in foreign currency. This repres ents 
about 35 to 40% of their total debt on average. And in fact, now, this figure has decreased over the past decade, and this 
contributes to their resilience. However, governments in emerging markets have a less favorable position as regards their 
debt profile and their financing costs are also on average higher.  
0:22:02 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And finally, just a few words about a larger subject, China.  
0:22:07 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
China's public debt is significant and growing rapidly. That being said, it is denominated in domestic currency and issued in  
domestic markets. This debt is, however, sustainable in the medium term due to a very low effective interest rate and a 
high growth rate. 
0:22:29 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Cynthia, for being with us today.  
0:22:32 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Thank you. 
0:22:33 - Emmanuel Laborde 
It is now time to conclude, and we welcome back Group Chief Economist Isabelle Mateos y Lago.  
It is time for our conclusion with Isabelle. Once again, just a final question, please. Could or should, I do not know which 
word is the good one, central banks intervene to curb this rise in interest rates?  
0:22:58 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Absolutely not. The main contribution that central banks can bring to dealing with this situation is to restore price stabili ty, 
as is their mandate, as promptly as possible. And in the current circumstances, this means keeping relatively high policy 
rates, in some cases, rising them further. And the responsibility for keeping public finances on a sustainable footing rests 
squarely with governments and any attempt by these governments to instead take the easy way out by pressuring central 
banks to lower interest rates prematurely would be enormously counterproductive.  
0:23:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Isabelle. Thank you all for joining us today. Just a few words to remind you that additional economic

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research

26 October 2026

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.5% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4.25% - 4.50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y in Q3 2026 (1.2% q/q) after 4.3% y/y in Q2 and 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a 
whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth remains characterised by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports 
and the fragility of sectors that rely on domestic demand has widened this year. Retail sales barely increased in the first nine months of 2026, 
the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting investment 
and private consumption will increase over the last months of the year . CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 3.0% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.5% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would improve slightly to 1.3% in 2026 and remain at this level in 2027  (1.2% in 2025). The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averag ing 3.2% in 2026 and 3.5% in 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting 
second-round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. 
The BoE is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

United States: Elevated inflation and high growth
02/10/2026 4
August 2026: Marked improvement in services
business confidence, from an already high level
September 2026: Manufacturing business

confidence remains stable at a high level
August 2026: Increasing inflationary pressures September 2026: Supplier deliveries do not get
longer
August 2026: Consumers are a bit more
concerned about their personal finances
August 2026: Headline inflation resists to the
upside
U.S. macroeconomic performance has remained solid since the onset of the shock, as illustrated in particular by Q2 growth
of +3.8% and +9% (annualized quarterly rate) in consumer spending and private nonresidential investment, respectively.

In the manufacturing sector, business sentiment has gained momentum despite the shock, while the impact of this shock
on the services sector has remained very limited. The immediate reaction of consumer confidence was, however, more
negative before beginning to improve, which was nevertheless interrupted in August (latest available data for households’
sentiment on their future personal financial situation). The University of Michigan’s composite indicator continued to

deteriorate in September, due to the sharp rise in fuel prices. But declining confidence still does not translate into reduced
spending—at least not at the aggregate level.
On the inflation front, the spike immediately following the outbreak of the conflict in Iran was more pronounced than in
the months following Russia’s invasion of Ukraine. The decline in June still left inflation at a high level, but its stability in
July and August was notable (3.4% y/y), as was the latest—the third consecutive—slight decline in inflation excluding

energy in August (2.5% y/y, same rate as in February). Given the existing inflationary pressures, however, inflation is highly
likely to resume its upward trend in September.
Source: BLS, ISM, NFIB, University of Michigan, BNP Paribas

## Document 412: US Daily_ Why Has the US Economy Been So Resilient to Higher Rates and Higher Oil Prices_

### Page 1

Abhay Duggirala

abhay.duggirala@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
US Daily: Why Has the US Economy Been So Resilient to Higher Rates 
and Higher Oil Prices?
28 September 2026 | 10:38AM EDT
We estimate that the economy grew at a 3.4% pace in Q3 and forecast 2.2% n
growth in 2026 overall on a Q4/Q4 basis, close to our estimate of potential GDP 
growth and only 0.3pp below our start-of-the-year forecast before the large 
increases in interest rates and oil prices. Why, we are often asked, has the US 
economy proven so resilient to these shocks? 
While interest rates have risen substantially this year, our financial conditions n
index framework implies that the economic impact should be nearly offset by the 
effects of higher equity prices, narrower credit spreads, and a weaker dollar, 
resulting in only a 0.1pp net hit to 2026 GDP growth. While the net impact of this 
year’s market moves on overall GDP is modest, the effects on some parts of the 
economy—especially the hit to housing—are more meaningful. 
We estimate that the increase in oil and refined product prices this year, together n
with the path our commodities strategists expect through year-end, will subtract 
0.3pp from 2026 growth. The drag comes from consumption as higher energy 
prices erode real purchasing power. While US energy producers were reluctant to 
boost investment when oil prices first rose, the increase in the rig count index in 
recent months points to some offsetting boost to GDP in Q3 and Q4. 
Our analysis suggests that slightly tighter financial conditions and higher oil n
prices should together be roughly a 0.4pp drag on GDP growth. The downgrade 
we have made to our forecast for the year, in part based on incoming data, is only 
slightly smaller than that, with the slight outperformance coming largely from 
consumer spending. This suggests that the economy’s resilience is less puzzling 
than it might appear at first.

### Page 2

Why Has the US Economy Been So Resilient to Higher Rates and Higher Oil Prices?

We now estimate that the economy grew at a 3.4% pace in Q3 and forecast 2.2% growth 
in 2026 overall on a Q4/Q4 basis, close to our 2.3% estimate of potential GDP growth 
and only 0.3pp below our start-of-the-year forecast when we did not expect large 
increases in interest rates and oil prices. In today’s Daily, we ask why the US economy has 
proven so resilient to these surprises. 
Part of the answer is that financial conditions have changed little on net since January, as 
the substantial rise in interest rates has been nearly offset by the effects of higher equity 
prices, narrower credit spreads, and a weaker dollar (Exhibit 1, left). Our estimated GDP 
growth impulse from the FCI is therefore little changed from the start of the year (Exhibit 
1, right), implying only a 0.1pp hit to 2026 Q4/Q4 GDP growth.

While the net impact of this year’s market moves on overall GDP is modest, the effects 
on some components of GDP are more meaningful. We use the Fed’s macroeconomic 
model FRB/US to estimate how the particular combination of market moves this year 
should affect each component of GDP . We find a meaningful hit from higher rates to 
residential investment, offset by slight boosts to consumption and business investment 
(Exhibit 2).

### Page 3

What about the effect of higher oil and refined products prices? Our rule of thumb 
implies that a sustained $10/barrel increase in oil prices lowers GDP growth by just over 
0.1pp through the consumption channel as higher energy costs erode real purchasing 
power. Applied to the path oil prices have taken this year, the unusual widening in 
refined product spreads, and our commodities strategists’ forecasts for prices through 
year-end, the rule of thumb implies a 0.4pp drag on GDP growth. 
While US oil producers were initially reluctant to respond to higher prices by increasing 
capex because of the uncertainty of the war with Iran and the outlook for oil prices, the 
increase in the rig count index in Q3 (Exhibit 3) points to a pickup in energy capex more 
recently. We estimate that the consumption drag and capex boost will net out to a 0.3pp 
hit to GDP growth from the oil shock. Two alternative approaches, FRB/US simulations 
and a model estimated using exogenous oil shocks, deliver similar estimates on average.1 
1 We use the series of exogenous oil shocks from Känzig (2021), “The Macroeconomic Effects of Oil Supply 
News: Evidence from OPEC Announcements” to estimate the effects of the current oil shock on GDP growth.

Sensitive to Higher Interest Rates

component under this year's changes in market conditions versus a counterfactual where
conditions were unchanged from the start of the year.

### Page 4

Our analysis suggests that slightly tighter financial conditions and higher oil prices 
should together be roughly a 0.4pp drag on GDP growth. The downgrade we have made 
to our forecast for the year, in part based on incoming data, is only slightly smaller than 
that, suggesting that the puzzle of economic resilience is smaller than it might appear at 
first. 
The economy’s slight outperformance relative to what we might have expected largely 
comes from consumer spending. At the start of the year, we forecasted consumption 
growth of 2.4% in 2026 Q4/Q4. Under the market and commodity price moves that 
actually materialized, our oil shock model implies a 0.6pp hit to that forecast, partially 
offset by a 0.2pp boost from higher equity prices in our wealth effects model, netting to 
an expected 2.0% growth in consumption this year. Against that, we now estimate—in 
part based on the surprisingly solid incoming data in recent months—that consumption 
will grow 2.2%, a modest upside surprise of 0.2pp.

## Document 439: 「関西の景気動向」９月号 AI関連需要の拡大と関西の製造業生産（PDF：841KB）

Source PDF: all report pdf/「関西の景気動向」９月号　AI関連需要の拡大と関西の製造業生産（PDF：841KB）.pdf
Report source: JRI
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

2026 年９月 
株式会社 日本総合研究所 
調査部 関西経済研究センター 
http://www.jri.co.jp/report/medium/kansai/

１．景気の現状と展望 
 関西の景気は、緩やかな回復基調を維持しつつも、一部に弱い動きがみられる。企業部門では、円安傾向に
加えて、世界的なAI関連需要の拡大を背景に輸出額は増加している。AI関連投資の活発化などを背景に、
鉱工業生産にも持ち直しの動きがみられる。設備投資は、中東情勢の混乱などによる先行き不透明感が意識
されるなかでも、企業の収益計画が高めの水準を維持するもとで拡大基調が続いている。一方、家計部門では、
所得環境の改善が続くものの物価高が重石となり、個人消費の回復ペースは緩慢にとどまっている。 
先行きを展望すると、中東情勢の混乱が長引くなか、資源価格の高止まりが企業活動の重石となる。一方、
世界的な AI 関連需要の拡大が関西の生産や輸出に波及する動きが続き、設備投資の押し上げにもつながる
と見込まれる。個人消費は、持続的な賃上げの動きが下支えに寄与するものの、物価高による下押し圧力が
根強く、回復ペースは引き続き緩慢にとどまる見通しである。

２．個別指標の動き 
（１） 輸出・生産動向 
関西の輸出額（円ベース）は大きく増加している。国・地域別にみると、中国（香港を含む）向けや、アジ
ア（除く中国・香港）向けの伸びが大きく、ともに半導体等電子部品の増加がけん引している。米国向けは振
れを伴いつつも増加傾向にある。品目別にみると、電気機器では半導体等電子部品、一般機械では半導体
等製造装置の増加が顕著にみられ、世界的な AI 関連需要の高まりが押し上げに寄与しているとみられる。金
属及び同製品も先端材料としての需要増や、それに伴う市況の高騰を背景に高水準で推移している。 
一方、輸出を数量面でみると、回復傾向にあるものの、ペースは緩やかにとどまっている。中国向けやアジア
NIEs 向けは低水準での推移が続いているほか、ASEAN 向けも伸び悩んでいる。米国向けは振れを伴いつつ
も、基調としては底堅く推移している。日本銀行が公表している実質輸出指数にも増勢の加速はみられない。

### Page 2

2 
以上のように、輸出数量の回復が緩慢にとどまる一方、輸出金額が大幅に増加しており、両者の動きに乖離
がみられる。これは、円安による輸出金額の押し上げや、AI 関連需要の拡大を受けた関連財の価格高騰によ
る影響が大きい。

鉱工業生産は、持ち直しの動きが続いている。業種別にみると、半導体製造装置やデータセンター向け設備
など AI 関連需要の裾野の広がりが追い風となっている生産用機械工業や電気・情報通信機械工業、機能性
素材や医薬品の増産を受けた化学工業の上振れが目立つ。一方、電子部品・デバイス工業は依然として低
水準での推移が続いている（関西における電子部品・デバイス生産の低迷について、詳しくは後述のトピックス
を参照）。

（２） 企業収益・設備投資動向 
企業収益を日本銀行大阪支店「短観」（６月調査、全規模・全産業）でみると、関西企業全体の 2026
年度の経常利益（全産業・全規模）は前年度比▲12.9％と、減益見通しとなった。中東情勢の混乱が長
引くなか資源価格の高止まりなどが収益の下押し圧力となっている。もっとも、2025 年度が同＋16.3％と、価
格転嫁の浸透などを背景に想定を大きく上回って着地したため、その反動減という側面も大きい。コロナ禍前と
の比較では、引き続き高めの水準を維持している。

企業が高水準の収益を見込むもとで、関西企業は積極的な設備投資姿勢を維持している。日本銀行大
阪支店「短観」６月調査による2026年度の設備投資計画（全産業・全規模）は前年度比＋9.7％と、過
去（2010～2024 年度）の平均伸び率を上回る水準にある。機械設備や建設資材の価格上昇などから名
目の投資額が押し上げられている点は割り引く必要があるものの、中東情勢の緊迫化が企業の設備投資マイ
ンドに与える影響は今のところ限定的にとどまっている。ソフトウエア投資も前年度比＋5.1％と増勢が続いてお
り、効率化や省力化を目的とした企業の積極的な投資姿勢に変化はみられない。

（３） 雇用・所得動向 
関西の雇用環境は、人手不足を背景に良好な状態が続いている。雇用関連指標をみると、有効求人倍率
は１倍を超え、完全失業率（季節調整値）は 2％台後半の低位で推移している。雇用者数は高めの水準
を保っている。 
所得面をみると、府県別「毎月勤労統計調査」から推計した４～６月期の関西の一人当たり現金給与総
額は前年同期比＋4.3％と、前期に比べてプラス幅が拡大した。今年の春闘における高い賃上げ水準での妥
結や、夏季賞与の増加（大阪府内企業を対象とする調査では前年比＋2.5％）などを反映し、賃金は高め
